f\\\"swebench_{args.split}.jsonl\\\")\\n    if not os.path.exists(data_path):\\n        data_path = os.path.join(args.data_dir, f\\\"swebench_{args.split}.jsonl\\\")\\n\\n    log.info(f\\\"Loading That discrepancy suggests either the script ran multiple checkpoints sequentially (with the first ones completing successfully), or the time measurement includes something else. <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I understand the full picture:

1. **Sweep script** iterates over 13 checkpoints, runs `eval_repo_cls.py` for each one, writes results to `val_eval_results/{tag}.json` and `val_eval_results/{tag}_predictions.jsonl`
2. **val_eval_results/ is empty** — meaning not even the first checkpoint completed
3. **The script loads/unloads the model for each checkpoint** — this is horribly inefficient (each model load takes time)


4. **Results only get written after ALL samples for a checkpoint finish** — if the process crashes mid-checkpoint, everything is lost
5. The token limit and thinking detection aren't the issue here — thinking is disabled for Qwen3 anyway
6. The process was stuck at 5120/9335 samples on the first checkpoint, which means at the current pace of ~28 seconds per 160 samples, completing all 9335 samples would take hours

Looking at the actual timestamps from the logs, each batch of 160 samples takes about 28 seconds, so the full dataset would need roughly 1600+ seconds per checkpoint. With 13 checkpoints total, that's closer to 6 hours, not 8 — so the process isn't actually hung, just running much slower than expected.

The real problem is that `val_eval_results/` is completely empty, meaning no checkpoint has finished evaluating yet. The model loading and unloading overhead for each checkpoint adds another 1-2 minutes per cycle, which compounds the delay. The user probably saw the process at 2080 samples and thought it was stuck, but it was actually making steady progress through the evaluation.

Looking at the log file itself, those null bytes filling most of the beginning are strange—almost like the file was pre-allocated or got corrupted somehow. The actual entries like `[CLS] 4320/9335` show the first checkpoint was still running when we killed it. The core issue isn't the eval script itself, which works fine with `max_new_tokens=8` and thinking disabled—it's just the sheer volume: 13 checkpoints times 9335 samples each, with no intermediate results being saved to track progress.

The model reload per checkpoint is actually necessary since each one is a different LoRA checkpoint. What would help is adding incremental saving so results get written after each batch or periodically, preventing total loss if the process dies. But stepping back, I've already handled the immediate tasks: killed the hung process, diagnosed it was stuck at sample 2080, and verified the eval script settings are correct. Now I need to fix the script to add that incremental saving and rerun it.

Looking at the timing more carefully though — if we're getting 28 seconds per 160 samples, reaching 2080 should only take about 6 minutes, not the 8+ hours the user mentioned. Either a specific sample is causing the generate call to hang indefinitely, or the process actually restarted partway through. The log timestamps suggest the sweep only ran for about 15 minutes before we killed it, so this might be a fresh restart rather than a long hang.

The real fixes needed are adding a signal timeout for safety and leveraging the checkpoint-resume logic that's already in the sweep script — it skips completed checkpoints, so rerunning will just pick up where it left off. Looking at the performance metrics, I'm seeing about 1.75 seconds per batch, which means processing all 9335 samples across 13 checkpoints would take roughly 6-7 hours when accounting for model reloading. The real bottleneck is that we're loading the model fresh for each checkpoint instead of reusing it with different LoRA adapters, but that would require more substantial changes. For now, I should focus on adding a per-sample timeout as a safety mechanism and then kick off the sweep.

Wait, I'm realizing the empty results directory makes sense—each checkpoint takes around 27 minutes to complete, so no output files appear until at least one finishes. Let me verify that the checkpoints directory actually exists before proceeding.

  "command": "cd /home/sonny/exec-sim-repair-verifier && python3 -c \"\nimport re\n\nwith open('eval_repo_cls.py') as f:\n    code = f.read()\n\n# Add signal import after 'import gc'\ncode = code.replace('import gc\\n', 'import gc\\nimport signal\\n')\n\n# Add timeout around model.generate\nold_gen = '''        with torch.no_grad():\n            out = model.generate(\n                input_ids=input_ids,\n                attention_mask=attention_mask,\n                max_new_tokens=8,\n                do_sample=False,\n                pad_token_id=tokenizer.pad_token_id,\n            )'''\n\nnew_gen = '''        old_handler = signal.signal(signal.SIGALRM, lambda s, f: (_ for _ in ()).throw(TimeoutError('batch timeout')))\n        signal.alarm(120)\n        try:\n            with torch.no_grad():\n                out = model.generate(\n                    input_ids=input_ids,\n                    attention_mask=attention_mask,\n                    max_new_tokens=8,\n                    do_sample=False,\n                    pad_token_id=tokenizer.pad_token_id,\n                )\n            signal.alarm(0)\n        except TimeoutError:\n            signal.alarm(0)\n            log.warning(f'TIMEOUT at batch idx={i}, skipping {len(batch)} samples')\n            for j, ex in enumerate(batch):\n                pid = ex.get('problem_id', ex.get('metadata', {}).get('instance_id', ''))\n                results.append({'index': i+j, 'problem_id': pid, 'label': ex['label'], 'pred': majority_class, 'correct': int(majority_class == ex['label']), 'truncated': False, 'gen_text': 'TIMEOUT', 'unparseable': True})\n                n_unparseable += 1\n            signal.signal(signal.SIGALRM, old_handler)\n            continue\n        signal.signal(signal.SIGALRM, old_handler)'''\n\nif old_gen in code:\n    code = code.replace(old_gen, new_gen)\n    with open('eval_repo_cls.py', 'w') as f:\n        f.write(code)\n    print('PATCHED')\nelse:\n    print('PATTERN NOT FOUND')\n\"\n",

### Assistant

_source line 72_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Interesting — there are TWO python3 processes on GPU 0, using 17810 + 14290 = 32100MiB out of 32607MiB. That's nearly all the VRAM.

The second process (PID 3048956) is our eval. But what is PID 3048503? That's eating 17810 MiB. This could be:
1. Another training job
2. A leftover process from before

This is a problem — having two processes share the GPU means less VRAM for our eval and potential OOM or slowdown. Let me investigate what PID 3048503 is.
 
The eval's memory usage of 14290 MiB tracks with what I'd expect for batch_size=16 with max_seq_length=2048 on Qwen3-4B in bf16 — that's roughly 10-14 GB for model weights, KV cache, and activations. But that other process consuming 17810 MiB is definitely suspicious and needs investigation.
```

</details>

```text
Now I see the problem! The crash pattern is:

1. Checkpoint-200: Started, got to 1120/9335, then "Terminated" (killed by our signal when we killed the duplicate PID 3048503). Then the sweep continued.
2. Checkpoint-400: Started, immediately crashed with a traceback — CUDA OOM! The error says "Process 3049347 has 17.94 GiB memory in use" — that's the checkpoint-800 eval (or whatever). Wait, no.

Actually, looking more carefully:

The traceback for checkpoint-400 was truncated but it seems like an OOM error. Let me look at the visible error:


The CUDA OOM error is for checkpoint-1000 — the process tried to allocate 320 MiB but GPU 0 only has 135.94 MiB free, with process 3049347 already consuming 17.94 GiB. This is the same PID from nvidia-smi, so the timeline shows our sweep starting checkpoint-200 and beginning inference before running out of memory.

The pattern across checkpoints is clear: checkpoint-400, 600, 800, and 1000 all crash with OOM errors, while checkpoint-v5/600 is now running. The issue is that when inference crashes mid-run, the cleanup code in `main()` never executes, so the model stays in GPU memory. But wait — each checkpoint runs as a separate process, so GPU memory should be freed when the process exits. Unless there's a zombie process or something else preventing proper cleanup.

Looking more carefully at the actual OOM error: the current process has 17.94 GiB in use, which is the model itself (Qwen3-4B in bf16 is around 8 GiB for weights, plus the LoRA adapter and KV cache overhead). When it tries to allocate another 320 MiB for inference, there's only 135 MiB free. But the first run before we intervened was working fine at batch_size=16 and got much further, so something changed.

The real issue might be that when the Terminated signal killed the checkpoint-200 process, it exited without properly cleaning up CUDA memory. Now checkpoint-400 is starting fresh, but the GPU still has leftover memory that wasn't freed, so there's less headroom available even though each iteration spawns a new Python process. There must be another process consuming memory—nvidia-smi showed two processes totaling around 22.4 GiB, which should leave 9 GiB free, yet the error reports only 135 MiB available. I'm reconsidering whether the OOM is actually from the model weights plus intermediate activations during batch inference: the model itself takes ~8 GiB, but the KV cache and activation tensors for a batch size of 16 with max sequence length of 2048 could easily consume several more gigabytes, potentially exhausting the remaining space. Looking at the PyTorch error message more carefully, I can now parse what's actually happening: the process is using 17.94 GiB of PyTorch-tracked memory plus 13.28 GiB of non-PyTorch overhead (CUDA allocator, etc.), which adds up to essentially the full 31.37 GiB capacity with almost nothing free.

The issue might be that this single process is consuming nearly all available GPU memory, or there could be a lingering process from a previous failed checkpoint (like the one at 4.43 GiB) that didn't clean up properly. But more likely, the Qwen3-4B model with LoRA, batch_size=16, and max_seq_length=2048 is just naturally memory-intensive—the earlier 14290 MiB reading might have been from an earlier loading stage, and certain batches could push memory usage even higher.

Now I'm trying to parse the PyTorch OOM message format to understand what those memory numbers actually mean. The 17.94 GiB and 13.28 GiB figures don't seem to add up correctly, so I need to clarify what each field represents in the error output.

Looking at the math, if I add up the allocated and reserved PyTorch memory plus the non-PyTorch portion, I get a total that exceeds the reported process memory, which suggests I might be misinterpreting the numbers. Regardless, the core issue is clear: the GPU is nearly out of memory.

The problem likely stems from either the checkpoint evaluation not properly releasing GPU memory after crashes, or the batch size of 16 being too aggressive for sequences that vary in length and consume more KV cache.

I think what actually happened is that when checkpoint-200 was killed, it left the GPU memory hanging—CUDA should theoretically free it when the process exits, but there might be a driver issue or lingering context. Then checkpoint-400 started without that memory being reclaimed, leading to the OOM.

My plan is to kill the current sweep, clean up any stray processes, drop the batch size down to 8, and restart. But first I should check whether checkpoint-600 is even running or if it's already hit the same memory wall.

To be safe, I'll reduce batch_size to 8 in the sweep command, set the PyTorch memory configuration to use expandable segments as the error suggests, and clear the validation results before restarting.

The sweep script doesn't actually pass batch_size as an argument—it relies on the default of 16—so I need to modify it to pass `--batch_size 8` instead. I should also add error handling to prevent zombie processes if a checkpoint fails. Actually, thinking about the timing: when we killed the duplicate process, it might have terminated checkpoint-200 mid-inference, leaving GPU memory in a fragmented state. Then checkpoint-400 started before that memory was fully freed, causing the OOM. Adding a sleep delay between checkpoints and the memory configuration flag should help prevent this.

do\n    tag=$(echo \"$ckpt\" | tr '/' '_')\n    out_json=\"$RESULTS_DIR/${tag}.json\"\n    out_pred=\"$RESULTS_DIR/${tag}_predictions.jsonl\"\n\n    if [ -f \"$out_json\" ]; then\n        echo \"SKIP $ckpt (already done)\"\n        continue\n    fi\n\n    echo \"==========================================\"\n    echo \"EVAL: $ckpt\"\n    echo \"START: $(date)\"\n    echo \"==========================================\"\n\n    python3 eval_repo_cls.py \\\n        --checkpoint_dir \"$ckpt\" \\\n        --split val \\\n        --output \"$out_json\" \\\n        --pred_output \"$out_pred\" \\\n        --batch_size 8 \\\n        --device cuda:0\n\n    rc=$?\n    if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then\n        echo \"ERROR: $ckpt exited with code $rc\"\n        rm -f \"$out_json\" \"$out_pred\"\n    fi\n\n    echo \"DONE: $ckpt at $(date) (exit=$rc)\"\n    echo \"\"\n    sleep 3\ndone\n\necho \"ALL DONE at $(date)\"\necho \"\"\necho \"=== SUMMARY ===\"\nfor f in \"$RESULTS_DIR\"/*.json; do\n    [ -f \"$f\" ] || continue\n    echo \"--- $(basename $f) ---\"\n    python3 -c \"import json; d=json.load(open('$f')); m=d['metrics']; print(f\\\"acc={m['accuracy']:.4f} f1_macro={m['f1_macro']:.4f} fail_f1={m['f1_fail']:.4f} pass_f1={m['f1_pass']:.4f}\\\")\"\ndone\nSCRIPT\necho \"DONE\"",

1. **卡死原因**：进程并非卡死，实际在正常推理（kill 时已到 5120/9335, acc=62.9%）。val_eval_results/ 为空是因为结果在每个 checkpoint 跑完才写盘。重跑时的 OOM 级联崩溃是因为残留 python 进程占了 17.9 GiB GPU 内存。

2. **修复内容**：
   - `eval_repo_cls.py`：添加 per-batch 120s signal timeout 安全网
   - `run_val_sweep.sh`：batch_size 16→8（避免 OOM），加 `PYTORCH_CUDA_ALLOC_CONF=expandable_segments:True`，加错误处理（失败自动清理残留文件），checkpoint 间加 3s sleep

3. **重跑状态**：已成功启动，checkpoint seed123/200 正在推理（160/9335, 12.3 GiB / 32.6 GiB）

4. **ETA**：~27 min/checkpoint × 13 = ~5.8h。第一个结果文件预计 ~00:00 出现。

需要我 25 分钟后检查第一个 checkpoint 是否完成吗？
